**Kenjiro Tsuda** (津田 健次郎, *Tsuda Kenjirō*; born June 11, 1971) is a Japanese actor, [voice actor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_acting_in_Japan), and film director, affiliated with the talent agency ANDSTIR.

## Early life

Tsuda lived in [Jakarta](16275.md) until he was seven years old.[^cite_note-キラキラACTORS_TV_{{!}}_キラキラWEB-2] He attended [Meiji University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meiji_University), where he majored in theater studies in the department of literature.[^cite_note-キラキラACTORS_TV_{{!}}_キラキラWEB-2] He wanted to become a film director when he became interested in the field of performance.[^cite_note-girls-style.jp-3] At the En • Theater Research Institute, he took and passed a non-degree course, being enthusiastic about theater work.[^cite_note-4]

## Career

After graduating from a trading school, Tsuda entered a [theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stage_\(theatre\)) group office for [auditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audition). He starred in the 1995 anime series *[H2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H2_\(manga\))*.[^cite_note-5] He had lived in poverty to the extent of agonizing over buying even a single piece of bread, but the work became a turning point for him.[^cite_note-6]

Tsuda likes both filming and watching movies. While in university, he joined a film making club. He attended movie theaters in junior high and watched western films until senior high, focusing on artistic films after entering college. However, his interest lies in non-genre films.[^cite_note-girls-style.jp-3]

In 2017, Tsuda replaced some of [Keiji Fujiwara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keiji_Fujiwara)'s roles in anime series, including *[Space Battleship Yamato 2202: Warriors of Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Battleship_Yamato_2202:_Warriors_of_Love)* and *[Attack on Titan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Titan_\(TV_series\))*.

On February 2, 2019, Tsuda debuted as a film director with the release of *Documentertainment AD-LIVE*, Documentertainment being a [portmanteau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portmanteau) of documentary and entertainment.[^cite_note-7]

On November 24, 2020, *Actor's Short Film* was launched by [Wowow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wowow) to commemorate the station's thirtieth year since opening. Five actors, facing the same conditions, such as budget and time, would individually direct short films up to 25 minutes long each. One of them would be chosen, based on the votes of viewers and film critics, to be presented at the Short Shorts Film Festival & Asia held in 2021[^cite_note-\:4-8] – which was announced on Twitter;[^cite_note-9] Tsuda starred in the short film *GET SET GO*, alongside [Ryo Ryusei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryo_Ryusei) and [Shunsuke Daito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shunsuke_Daito).[^cite_note-\:4-8][^cite_note-10] Distribution of the films began on January 13, 2021.[^cite_note-11] He was announced to voice Figarland Shamrock for *[One Piece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Piece_\(1999_TV_series\))*, it was announced in the promotional video for *[One Piece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Piece)* Volume 113.

Tsuda made a cameo appearance in the [morning drama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asadora) *[Yell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yell_\(TV_series\))*.[^cite_note-12] Since then, he has achieved success on series such as *Dearest* (2021) and *[Ōoku: The Inner Chambers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%8Coku:_The_Inner_Chambers)* and *Reversal Orchestra*, both in 2023.[^cite_note-13] In 2023, his photo book recorded second place, unusual for a photo book of a voice actor over fifty years old.[^cite_note-14]

In November 2025, Tsuda filed a lawsuit in Tokyo against [TikTok](56822861.md) for alleged unauthorized of his voice in [AI-generated speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI-generated_speech) in videos on the platform and sought the removal of videos containing such voice. Tsuda's case is centered on it being an exploitation of his publicity rights while TikTok claimed that it is "a generic male voice that lacks a distinct speaking style, arguing that Tsuda's claims are entirely subjective". As of May 2026, the case was ongoing.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16] He later won the case that fall. [^cite_note-17]

## Personal life

Tsuda married a woman he encountered around when he was starting out as a stage actor, who he mentioned in his [Instagram](31591547.md) announcement to not be a celebrity,[^cite_note-18] and they have two children. By not officially announcing his marriage, Tsuda thought that he could protect his family. Another reason for this approach is the large appeal that his favorite actors have carried as figures who have not displayed their private lives, and he had thoughts of wanting to become an expressive person like that. He never made his marriage public; however, in July 2020, he received notice that a news story about his marriage had been published in one section of the weekly magazine [Shūkanshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sh%C5%ABkanshi) and decided to bring these facts to light via his Instagram.[^cite_note-19]
